# LOG reverse engineering bible

The single index of everything known about the ROMs. Every fact is one line with search tags. Detail and evidence live in the linked source document.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **Rule:** every session that learns a fact adds one line here in the same session, with its label and its source. A fact that is not in this file is treated as unknown.

---

## 1. How to search

Use these tag shapes. Each one is unique, so a plain text search finds every line about it.

| Search for | Finds |
|---|---|
| `@0x0801F394` | A GBA address (code, ROM data as `@0x08xxxxxx`, RAM as `@0x02xxxxxx` or `@0x03xxxxxx`) |
| `rom:0x3B4E90` | A file offset in the ROM (address minus `0x08000000`) |
| `flag:195` | A story flag |
| `set:56` | A sprite set id |
| `pal:88` | A shared OBJ palette index |
| `char:gohan` | A character (`gohan`, `piccolo`, `vegeta`, `trunks`, `goku`, `hercule`) |
| `#codec` `#sprite` `#save` `#form` `#story` `#map` `#palette` `#hud` `#status` `#portrait` `#text` `#script` `#antipiracy` `#debug` `#tool` `#decision` `#open` `#boot` `#object` `#state` `#combat` `#item` `#menu` `#input` `#sound` | A topic |
| `game:bf` `game:log2eu` | A fact about Buu's Fury or the Europe LoG II. No game tag means LoG II USA |

Labels: **C** = CONFIRMED (proved, evidence in the source doc), **L** = LIKELY, **S** = SPECULATIVE, **O** = my own statement (from memory or play, not checked in the ROM).

| EU scene tables and their USA twins (the record words map through the runs; votes in `usa_tables.txt`): Goku vs 19 EU `rom:0x76F80C` = USA `rom:0x3D40C8`; prologue EU `rom:0x6EF6B0` = USA `rom:0x3B7ADC`; chamber exit EU `rom:0x78F9EC` = USA `rom:0x3DBDD4`; map 14,3 Jr tables EU `rom:0x7CC0DC`, `0x7CC13C`, `0x7CD790` = USA `rom:0x3E9E24`, `0x3E9E84`, `0x3EA400`; self-destruct EU `rom:0x7D25B8` = USA `rom:0x3EB704`; post-Cell EU `rom:0x7D553C` = USA `rom:0x3EC758`; Goku meets Trunks EU `rom:0x70CADC`, `0x713224` = USA `rom:0x3BE798`, `0x3BFDDC`; epilogue EU `rom:0x6F0A14` = USA `rom:0x3B8140` | #script #story game:log2eu | C (3 or more votes), L (1 vote: epilogue, the two first Jr tables, Vegeta start `rom:0x3D21F0`) | ZHK |

## 3. Codec and graphics storage `#codec`

| Fact | Tags | Label | Src |
|---|---|---|---|
| Decompressor: ARM routine at `@0x03000000` (IWRAM), `decompress(r0 = dst, r1 = src)` | #codec | C | SPR |
| Decompressor source bytes in ROM at `rom:0x7FD6BC`; crt0 copy triple at `rom:0x148` (`087FD6BC 03000000 00000E7F`) | #codec | C (bytes), L (copy) | SPR |
| Bit reader subroutine `@0x0300024C` (read r8 bits into r7, MSB first) | #codec | C | SPR |
| Thumb-to-ARM veneer to the decompressor `@0x080236A8`; 14 BL call sites | #codec | C | SPR |
| Frame unpack dispatcher `@0x0801F394`: header `{u32 type, u32 size, stream at +8}`; type 0 raw copy via `@0x08021DF0`, type 1 or 2 compressed; 72 callers (sprites, backgrounds, UI) | #codec #sprite | C | SPR |
| Second dispatcher with remap table `@0x0801F3BA`, `@0x0300029C` | #codec | C (code), L (purpose) | SPR |
| Stream format: 32-bit LE words, MSB first; full code table in SPR "Stream format" | #codec | C | SPR |
| No BIOS LZ77 blocks exist in the ROM; BIOS RLE hits are false positives | #codec | C | RN |
| No compressor exists yet. Slice 1 uses raw type 0 frames instead | #codec #decision #open | C | SPR, DES |
| Tool: `scripts\permanent\rom\webfoot_decompress.py` (`frame`, `find`, `set`) | #tool #codec | C | SPR |
| Buu's Fury uses the same frame codec: 12,457 of 12,471 aligned type 1/2 headers decode to exactly their size with `webfoot_decompress` (LoG II 12,325 of 12,334) | #codec game:bf | C | BFS |
| Raw run code `001 0000 1` copies 256 bytes of bits(8) per continue bit: ARM `@0x03000088` `mov r9,#0x100`, loop `@0x0300008C`-`@0x0300009C`, continue bit `@0x030000A0`-`@0x030000B0` (`bcs 0x03000088`). The Buu's Fury decomp decoder `webfoot_lz.py:187-192` reads 2 bytes per continue bit, so it disagrees with the LoG II ARM routine; our `webfoot_decompress.py:136-139` agrees | #codec | C (disassembly `scripts\one-time\2026-10-16-first-probes\dis_iwram.txt`) | TRV |
| Nibble code `001` + bits(4) = 1 writes byte 0, not the literal base: `@0x03000064` read 4 bits, `@0x03000068` `subs r7,r7,#1`, `@0x0300006C` `beq 0x03000020` stores r7 = 0. The decomp decoder `webfoot_lz.py:177-184` writes `literal_base` there (a third difference; reached only after a 7-bit literal mode with a nonzero base) | #codec | C (disassembly) | TRV |
| Literal mode at entry: the decompressor `@0x03000000` sets r5, r10, r12 only; through the dispatcher `@0x0801F394` r3 still holds the frame type (`ldr r3,[r2,#0]`, compares, `bl 0x080236A8`; the veneer does not touch r3), so a literal sent before the mode code reads type-many bits (1 or 2) plus the caller's r4. `webfoot_lz.py:253-258` models the width this way with base 0; our decoder raises instead. No walked LoG II frame sends a literal first | #codec | C (code), L (no stream relies on it) | TRV |
| The frame bitstream is the JCALG1 bitstream with a Webfoot container. Upstream `jeremycollake/jcalg1` `jccomp.inc`: `INITIAL_BASE equ 8`, `DecrementIndex1 = 027fh`, `DecrementIndex2 = 037ffh`, `DecrementIndex3 = 010000h`, `SHORT_RANGE = 1111111b`, `SHORT_BITS = 7`, `ONEBYTE_PHRASE_BITS = 4`, `LITERAL_BITSIZE = 1`, `BLOCK_SIZE equ 256`: each equals the LoG II ARM routine (offset width 8, length bonus thresholds, 7-bit short match, 4-bit one-byte phrase, 7 + 1-bit literal width, 256-byte raw block). JCALG1's own header is `{u16 'JC', u32 size, u32 checksum}`; LoG II uses `{u32 type, u32 size}` instead. Raises row "codec identity LIKELY" in section 10a | #codec #community | C (constants and tag set), L (bit-for-bit identity; jcalg1_d.asm read as a summary only) | TRV |
| A compressor exists and round-trips: `pack(payload, kind=1)` from the Buu's Fury decomp (`tools\webfoot\webfoot_lz.py:375-479`, MPL-2.0, copy `scripts\one-time\2026-10-22-modder-tool-review\vendor\webfoot_lz_pack.py`) decodes with `webfoot_decompress` to the exact input for all 1,656 r27 frames tried (96 dialog portraits: 86 type 1, 10 type 0; 1,560 unique sprite frames: sets 53-61, 103, 157-160 in full, every 25th of the rest) and 6 synthetic payloads (0 to 70,000 bytes). It emits only literal mode 8-bit base 0, literals, one-byte phrases, short matches, repeat-offset and long matches with offset width 8, and the end marker. Size: 104.3 % of the original type 1 portrait streams, 106.1 % of the sprite streams, 26.2 % of the raw type 0 portraits. Supersedes "No compressor exists yet" | #codec #tool #decision | C (round trip, `scripts\one-time\2026-10-22-modder-tool-review\roundtrip.py`) | TRV |
| `pack()` defaults to type 2 (`kind=PACKED_KINDS[1]`); call it with `kind=1`, because the remap dispatcher `@0x0801F3BA` does not unpack type 2 | #codec #tool | C (code) | TRV |
| Frame dispatcher `@0x0801F394`: type 0 calls `@0x08021DF0` (ARM memcpy at `0x08021DF4`: `cmp r2,#3`, byte path for short or odd lengths) with size = header +4; types 1 and 2 call `@0x080236A8` (`bx pc`, then `ldr pc,[pc,#-4]` to IWRAM `0x03000000`) on header + 8. The animated group load `@0x0800501E` calls the same `@0x080236A8` on frame + 4 | #codec | C (listings) | BMT |
| The packer `scripts\permanent\rom\webfoot_lz_pack.py` (copy of the TRV vendor file, code not edited) starts every stream with the 9 bits `001 0000 0 1` (nibble code 0, not a raw run, 8-bit literal mode), so the decoder's entry r3 (0 on the group path, the frame type on the dispatcher path) does not matter. Its streams are 4-byte multiples | #codec #tool | C (pack source; `test_every_stream_sets_literal_mode_first`) | BMT |

## 4. Sprites `#sprite`

| Fact | Tags | Label | Src |
|---|---|---|---|
| OBJ mapping 1D; every sprite is 8bpp with one shared 256-color OBJ palette | #sprite #palette | C | RN |
| Sprite set table `@0x083B4E90` / `rom:0x3B4E90`; set id N >= 7 uses entry N-7, lookup at `@0x08009364` | #sprite | C | SPR |
| Set ids 1-6 resolve through a run-time table of 28-byte records at `@0x08009350` (L: the six party slots). Superseded by the next line | #sprite | L | SPR |
| `get_set(id)` `@0x08009324`: id 0 = player object +0x48 (`@0x08009334`), or the leader's appearance when no player exists; ids 1-6 = character record id-1 (struct `@0x03000E90`, from the word at `rom:0x3B5C98`), then appearance +1 through `@0x0802645C`; id >= 7 = set table | #sprite #form | C | OUT |
| Set table `rom:0x3B4E90` ends at `rom:0x3B50E7`; `rom:0x3B50E8` is another table (literals `@0x08008014`, `@0x080082B4`, `@0x080085F8`). The set table literal `@0x0800959C` (bytes `90 4E 3B 08`, ldr `@0x08009364`) is its only reference, so new set ids need a relocated copy. Set id is a u8 | #sprite #decision | C | OUT |
| Record lookup correction: `@0x0800775A` reads `row = obj+0x4C`, `list = row[obj+0x10]`. obj+0x4C = animation row (`set + 12 + 16*dirs + anim*dirs*4`), obj+0x10 = direction | #sprite | C | OUT |

| Set header u16 +0 = the object's OBJ VRAM slot and heap buffer size in bytes (`@0x080075B0` `ldrh r1,[r1,#0]`, `@0x080075BC` `bl 0x080067ac`, `@0x080075DE`); byte +1 is its high byte, not a direction count; the game hardcodes 4 directions (`@0x08007568` set+0x4C). All 150 sets: no record exceeds the u16 (0x400 x77, 0x200 x54, 0x1000 x15). `set:53` to `set:59` = 0x400 | #sprite char:gohan | C | T5P2 |

| `chapter_cards` card fade (v2-v6): BLDCNT 0x2048 and BLDALPHA EVA = frame x 41 >> 8 in, (450 - frame) x 41 >> 8 out. EVA has 17 levels (0-16), so the picture changes on 16 of 100 frames, each level held 6 or 7 frames, 85-103 of the 105 shown entries at once, up to 2 of 31 levels per channel. BF's palette fade changes on 97 (in) and 94 (out) of 100 frames, at most 1 level, 7-89 entries per frame. Cause of my "fades are very jaggy" (2026-10-30). v1 used the same EVA method over 16 frames (one level per frame); v2 stretched it to 100 | #palette #patch #story | C (code, `test_tick_has_bf_timing`, `fade_frames.txt`), L (blend output min(31, c x EVA >> 4), GBATEK) | CHF |

| `chapter_cards` v7 card fade = BF's palette fades on the BG palette only (option B, variant B8 of the CHF report). Enter: BLDCNT 0, BLDALPHA 0, BG palette black (`scale(0)`). Fade in, frames 1-99: 256 BG words = BF fade table row x L >> 11, L = (frame << 8) / 100 (BF ext 121 rule, `@0x08007074`); rows = BF `rom:0x701B0` rows 0-255 with 247 and 248 from rows 257 and 258 (OBJ 1, 2), stored as a 0x300-byte data chunk (r40 v7 `rom:0x939F54`). Frame 100 and the hold: the stored BACKDROP palette bytes (bit 15 kept). Fade out, frames 351-450: 5-bit channel x L >> 8, L = 256 - ((frame - 350) << 8) / 100 (BF ext 120 rule, `@0x080176D6`); frame 450 black, tick returns 1. OBJ palette never written | #palette #patch #story | C (`fadefix_frames.txt`: interpreter run, 450 of 450 frames equal the closed form; `test_tick_has_bf_timing`) | CHF2 |

Plain option B (5-bit channel x L >> 8 for the fade in too) changed on 92 of 100 frames and differed from BF on 1065 entry-frames by 1 level (plain-B numbers: an earlier run of `fadefix_frames.py` on the plain-B module, before B8; that listing was overwritten and not kept) | #palette #patch | C (`fadefix_frames.txt`, `test_fades_step_on_bf_frames_by_one_level`) | CHF2 |

| Portrait table block `rom:0x3EC9D0`: word 0 `0x03000E90` (read for ids 1-7), then 115 words for ids 0-114 (0-7 are 0). The old block stays unchanged: readers at `@0x0800BBB4`, `@0x0800B86E`, `@0x0800BDB8` use nearby base addresses (index >= 5 at 0x0800BBB4 reaches the block). Never free or reuse `rom:0x3EC9D0`-`0x3ECBA3` | #portrait | C | POR |

## 7. Save format `#save`

| Fact | Tags | Label | Src |
|---|---|---|---|
| EEPROM library `@0x080218E0` (identify), `@0x08021ACC` (read dword), `@0x08021CB0` (program dword); game uses 8 KB | #save | C | SAV |
| RAM shadow of the EEPROM at `@0x0203E000` (pointer at `@0x03003898`); game uses bytes 0x000-0x447 | #save | C | SAV |
| Checksum: Adler-32 `@0x0802153E`, mod 65521 at `@0x08027F1C` | #save | C | SAV |
| Options block at EEPROM 0x000 (4 bytes) + `adler32 + 0x12345678`; defaults 4, 6, 4, 0 | #save | C | SAV |
| Slots at 0x10 + n*0x168: 0x164 bytes of the struct at `@0x03000E90` + u32 Adler-32 | #save | C | SAV |
| .sav file: each 8-byte block is byte-reversed against RAM | #save | C (I loaded all 3 test slots, 2026-10-16) | SAV, DES |
| Slot load `@0x08003F9C`, slot write `@0x08003FEC`, save game `@0x08004030`, continue `@0x08001848` | #save | C | SAV |
| Struct +0x00 leader (0 Gohan, 1 Piccolo, 2 Vegeta, 3 Trunks, 4 Goku, 5 Hercule) | #save | C | SAV |
| Struct +0x02/+0x03/+0x04 map zone, area, variation; +0x160/+0x162 player x, y | #save #map | C | SAV |
| Struct +0x0C: 6 character records of 28 bytes (HP, max HP, EP, max EP, appearance, attack slot, STR/POW/END 8.8, EXP, flags) | #save | C | SAV |
| Record +0x18..+0x1B = ability id per slot (Gohan `04 03 0c 02`); struct +0x08 = transform cooldown (+8 per frame of the Saiyan transform, -1 per frame in base form) | #save #form | C | FRM |
| Struct +0xB4: story flags 0-399 (50 bytes); +0xE7 visited maps; +0x11A Scouter; +0x12B item counts | #save #story | C | SAV |
| Struct `@0x03000E90` is fixed: the ROM table `@0x086F7E50` holds it at +4 (`rom:0x6F7E54` = `90 0E 00 03`). Flag test `@0x08003E42`: byte struct +0xB4 + n / 8, bit n & 7, so `flag:195` = `@0x03000F5C` bit 3 | #save #story | C | T6 |
| Level = from EXP table `rom:0x1D9834` (50 entries, cap 50). Level 40 needs EXP 2,727,775 | #save | C | SAV |
| Level-up routine `@0x08003CA0`; stats random per level, ranges at `rom:0x1D95F0`; stat cap 100 | #save | C | SAV |
| Tool: `scripts\permanent\rom\log2_save.py` (`info`, `build`, `set`) | #tool #save | C | SAV |
| Test save `test-data\saves\log2_lv40_postcell.sav`, SHA-1 `3ecf1e40...`: post-Cell, all six at level 40; slot 1 Capsule Corp save point, slot 2 East District 439 save point, slot 3 Capsule Corp post-Cell spawn | #save #tool | C (I loaded all 3) | SAV, DES |
| ext 98 (char, level) `@0x0800AC10`: cap 50; if below, level-up `@0x08003CA0(struct, char, cur, target)` then EXP = target threshold (`@0x08004276`). Level-up per level: max HP x (1 + (0xE14 + rand 0x51F) / 65536), max EP + 2-4, both refilled; stats += `lo*n + rand((hi-lo+1)*n)` 8.8 from `rom:0x1D95F0` (12 bytes per character), cap 100 (`@0x08003C6E`) | #save #script | C | CFS |

Messages (`@0x08010628`, +0x24): 9 forces a repaint, 0xA calls vtable +8, 0xF clears the target when it matches (enemy death broadcast), 0x10 slides out | #hud #combat #object | C (listings) | HKP |

| Script VM compare and branch ops: op 15 (`lt`, `@0x080090CA`) pops b, then replaces a with `a < b` (`cmp r3,r2 ; blt`), so `push a ; push b ; lt` = a < b. Op 19 (`jz`, `@0x0800910A`) pops a value, reads one offset byte and adds it to the pointer after that byte when the value is 0. Op 21 (`inc varN`, `@0x08009130`) calls ext N (the getter), pops the value, adds 1 and calls setter N (`rom:0x3B5F70` +4N); the stack is unchanged | #script | C (listing) | CJT |

| Find by set: `@0x08009370` returns `[@0x03001FA8]` for 0, else `@0x08006E0A` (world, 5, set object). `@0x08006E0A` walks world list 1 from index count-1 down to 0, then list 2 (+0x40C), and stops at the first object that answers. `world_add` `@0x080068B8` appends at index count. | BF card font: object `0x087548DC`, +0 = glyph table `0x08753A20`. Glyph = u16 index per char (< 0xFF; chars >= 0xFF use the object's +4/+8 fields); at the index: u16 width, then one u16 column per pixel, bit 0 at the top. Capitals use rows 1-15, small caps rows 4-13. Width = sum (width + 1) (`0x0803FFE8`). `.` and `'` are blank glyphs (width 2, no bits) | game:bf #text #font | C (bytes, listing) | CH3 |
| BF glyph draw `0x0803F70C`: fill colour (text context +3 = 2) where the column bit is set, else outline colour (+2 = 1) where a 4-neighbour bit is set (left, right, up, down; no diagonals). Column c lands at pen + c; outline columns at pen - 1 and pen + width; 17 rows. `0x0802BECC`: buffer W = (width + 33) & ~31 by 32 rows, pen x = W/2 - (width + 1) / 2 (mode 1, centred), pen y = 10, OBJ at (x - W/2, y - 16). So bit b of column c is on screen at (x - (width + 1) // 2 + c, y - 6 + b) | game:bf #text #font | C (listing), C (screenshot: 8 of 11424 text-band pixels off) | CH3 |

| ext 23 (`@0x08009628`) = visited test: pops the top value into r2 (area) and the next into r1 (zone), calls `@0x080041A6`(struct `[0x083B5C78 + 0x20]` = `0x03000E90`, zone, area) and pushes 1 when the map's visited bit is set, else 0. So `push 8,1 ; ext 23` pushes the 8,1 bit. No scene record calls ext 23 (record walk over the script region) and no `push ... ; 02 17` byte shape is a script | #script #map #save | C (listing), L (no script user: two shapes) | CH8 |

| The card 4 arrival on the v10 ROM runs as designed in all three branches: all 18 spots and facings equal the module's `SPOTS`, `ARRIVE` and `PLAYER_FACE`, read both at the arrival frame and 200 frames later with the first line open. A spot is final as the actor lands; a facing is applied later, one actor at a time. Reading the six facing bytes every frame in the Gohan branch: Trunks turns right at frame 3925, Vegeta left at 3927, the player right at 3929, each from the fly-in default (up for an actor, down for the player). All final from 3929; the first text box starts at about 3935. | Script base ops 4-16 are the arithmetic and comparison set, read from their handlers in the base op table `rom:0x3B5C00`. Each one decrements the stack count, reads the top and the value below it and stores the result in place: 4 and (`@0x08008FC7`, `cmp`/`beq`), 5 or (`@0x08008FE7`, `orr`), 6 neg (`@0x08009001`, the one unary op: it pops nothing), 7 add (`@0x0800900F`), 8 sub (`@0x08009023`, `sub r1,r2,r1` = second minus top), 9 mul (`@0x08009037`), 10 div (`@0x0800904B`, `bl @0x08021F30`), 11 eq (`@0x08009063`, `beq`), 12 ne (`@0x0800907D`, `bne`), 13 gt (`@0x08009097`, `bgt`), 14 ge (`@0x080090B1`, `bge`), 15 lt (`@0x080090CB`, `blt`), 16 le (`@0x080090E5`, `ble`). The comparison is second-from-top against top. These are BASE ops: the section 8 row naming "ext 4-7" as variable reads is about the ext numbering and does not conflict | #script | C (handler listings), L (the divide operand order: the handler passes the top in r0) | TLS |
| Script base ops 17-20, 27 and 28, from their handlers. Table word 17 is 0, so the interpreter ends a record itself. Op 18 (`@0x080090FF`) is the UNCONDITIONAL forward jump: its handler reads no stack value at all. Op 19 (`@0x0800910B`) pops one value and jumps forward only when that value is zero. Op 20 (`@0x08009129`) pops one value and discards it. Op 27 (`@0x08009251`) pops a count into the loop area at +0x44, and op 28 (`@0x0800926D`) counts it down and jumps back while it is non-zero. Ops 18 and 19 are easy to swap, and swapping them makes every branch read backwards | #script | C (handler listings) | TLS |
| A type 0 record can hold more than one op 17, so a decoder must not stop at the first one. Record `rom:0x3EC678` (post-Cell table `rom:0x3EC758`, index 49) runs wait 60, fade out 240, `ext 86 (1)`, the `ext 35` map load to 6,3,1 at (376,408) at `rom:0x3EC690`, wait 60, sound 47, `ext 29 (5)`, wait 80, SET 195 at `rom:0x3EC6A4`, then tests flags 305, 302, 306 and 303 joined by base op 4 (and), then `jz` to `push 53 ; end`, else `push 50 ; end`. Its first op 17 sits 3 bytes before the real end. Safe rule: stop at the first op 17 at or past the furthest target of any forward jump seen so far. It is the only such record in the eight tables walked (`rom:0x3DBDD4`, `0x3E8D98`, `0x3E93E8`, `0x3EC758`, `0x3DD044`, `0x3CF3A0`, `0x3D40C8`, `0x3E6078`) | #script #story flag:195 | C (decode; the SET 195 and map load addresses match the existing flag 195 row) | TLS |

Push counts per ext op come from a walk of tables `rom:0x3DBDD4`, `0x3E8D98`, `0x3E93E8`, `0x3EC758` and `0x3DD044` | #script | C (push counts over 5 tables, the flag 195 row), L (`ext 38`'s second form) | TLS |

| The compact push op `1d` takes a count byte and then one zigzag varint a value (`scene_lib.zigzag`, moved there from `piccolo_gi_scene`): u = v * 2 for v >= 0 and (-v) * 2 + 1 for v < 0, then 7 bits a byte, most significant group first, every group but the last carrying 0x80. So 8 gives `10`, 24 gives `30`, 84 gives `81 28`, 128 gives `82 00` and 272 gives `84 20`. Bit 0 of the first group is the sign, which is how the card 4 exit records push `OFF_Y` = -40. The one-value form is `00 nn`, used when a single value is 0 to 255, and it writes the raw byte | #script | C (`scripts\permanent\scene\scene_lib.py` `zigzag` and `push`, plus every push in `chapter_cards` record 0 decoded by hand) | C4M |

| The ext 55 handler `@0x08009EFC` binds its actor the same way ext 52 does: `bl 0x08009370` (find by set) at `@0x08009F14`, then `bl 0x0801F08C` and `bl 0x0800E04C` (actor queue) at `@0x08009F36`. The ext 52 handler `@0x08009D1C` has the identical call shape at `@0x08009D24`, `@0x08009D2C` and `@0x08009D44`. So `ext 55 (set, dir)` binds the newest live actor of that set, and a scene can turn an NPC that `ext 84` built from a map spawn item | #script #object #sprite | C (BL decode of both handlers in the v9 ROM) | CJA |
| The face-the-player step tick `@0x08010038` decides as follows (bytes `ac4207db002a02dd03220a7409e002220a7406e0002b02dd00220a7401e001220a74` at rom:0x1006A). It reads the player `[0x03001FA8]`, takes dx and dy as player +0x158/+0x15C minus NPC +0x158/+0x15C, then `cmp r4,r5 ; blt`, where r4 is \|dx\| and r5 is \|dy\|. When \|dx\| >= \|dy\| it goes horizontal: facing 3 when dx > 0, else 2. When \|dx\| < \|dy\| it goes vertical: facing 0 when dy > 0, else 1. So an exact diagonal falls to the horizontal branch. It stores the result at NPC +0x10 every frame it runs | #script #object #sprite | C (hand decode of the 34 bytes, pinned by `test_every_scripted_facing_is_the_one_the_step_picks`) | CJA |

| `github.com/ghxstboyy/Sprite-Swapper-Image-Extractor-for-Buu-s-Fury-and-Legacy-Of-Goku` (Ghost, based on Fralacticus): extracts sprites to BMP and reinserts them into LoG II and Buu's Fury, compressing with `jcalg1.exe`. So the frame codec (`#codec`, `@0x0801F394`) is likely JCALG1, and a compressor exists. Credits "Zhacks" for inserting sprites larger than the originals (compare the 0x400 VRAM slot, Ruling 35). Each tool carries a `Gba/JCALG1.cs` decoder, a second community source that names the frame codec JCALG1. `github.com/mainant/LOGExtractor` is an older copy, 3 commits behind | #community #tool #codec game:bf #map | O | me, 2026-10-17 |
| `github.com/ghxstboyy/webfootaudioengine` (Python plus NumPy, no license): scans Webfoot GBA music and SFX tables, exports songs as `.it` and SFX as `.wav`, imports `.it`/`.wav`, and imports songs from one ROM into another (add-slot relocates the table). Two music descriptor formats: 12-byte "LOG1Format" and 20-byte "WebfootFormat". Compare the music table `0x081D4B2C` (Research 3). Candidate tool for moving Buu's Fury music into the merged game. NumPy is not installed on this PC (I install if wanted) | #community #tool #sound game:bf | O | me, 2026-10-17 |

| `webfootaudioengine` 1bb0085 allocates every song, aux, sample bank and sample table by appending past the end of the input file (`rom_support\shared.py:36-39` `_reserve_append_space`); it never searches for 0xFF runs. On a 16 MB build the output grows past 16 MB. Add-slot also rewrites every byte-offset match of a descriptor pointer (`debug_patches.py:72-75`; r27: 7 aligned Thumb literal words, songs 0 x3, 15 x3, 21 x1, all in the base 8 MB), fills the old table `rom:0x1D4B2C` (880 bytes) with 0xFF (`table_ops.py:217-220`) and raises the sound test bound byte `rom:0x58F4` (0x2B) | #community #tool #sound | C (code, r27 scan) | TRV |

| Capture loop `scripts\permanent\emu\`: `capture.py` runs one job file through `EmuHawk.exe --config=<copy> --chromeless --lua=<run.lua> <rom.gba>` with a timeout, and `capture.lua` runs the job's steps (`wait`, `press`, `shot`, `read`, `savestate`, `end`) and writes `result.txt`. The wrapper refuses a ROM outside `builds\`, refuses a `savestate` path that already exists, seeds our `.sav` as BizHawk's SaveRAM, and lists every install file newer than its own marker after each run. `actors.py` walks the world object list in the RAM dumps and prints each actor's spot, facing and set | #tool | C (31 tests, 10 of 10 mutations caught, `capture_tests.txt`, `teeth.txt`) | CAP |
| BizHawk input: the GBA core has one controller and no `P1 ` prefix, so `joypad.get(1)` is empty and `joypad.set({A = true}, 1)` sets nothing. `joypad.set({A = true})` with no controller number works. This corrects the 2026-10-30 11:00 PM finding that input worked with the controller number | #tool #input | C (`probe_input.lua`: `joypad.get()` lists A, B, Up, Down, Left, Right, L, R, Start, Select, Power, Light Sensor, Tilt X, Y, Z; `joypad.get(1)` lists nothing; only the no-number form left the title) | CAP |
| BizHawk speed: the config key `Unthrottled` frees the speed, not `SpeedPercent` and not `client.speedmode`. ## 12. Final decisions `#decision`

Short form. Full wording in DES.

| Decision | Tags | Date |
|---|---|---|
| Base ROM is LoG II USA; English HP and ki scale | #decision | 2026-10-16 |
| Gohan wears the purple gi when playable after the Time Chamber; cutscenes before that keep the armor | #decision char:gohan | 2026-10-16 |
| After Gohan first has the gi, every later cutscene and NPC Gohan uses the gi (Cell Games Arena NPC, post-Cell ext 73 (0,56) scene). Deferred until slice 1 is tested; slice 1 leaves them in armor. Built in r16 (`npc_gi`, NGI) | #decision char:gohan set:56 #script | 2026-10-16 |
| Gohan wears the armor (sprite and portrait) in every scene up to the end of the chamber-exit cutscene; from the moment the player controls him after the chamber, the gi everywhere (playable, NPC, portrait); all Cell Games scenes are gi. ## 14. Engine map `#boot` `#object` `#state` `#combat` `#item` `#menu` `#input` `#sound`

One line per fact from research 3. Detail, tables and listings in OVW and `scripts\one-time\2026-10-17-engine-overview\`.

| Fact | Tags | Label | Src |
|---|---|---|---|
| Header `b 0x080000C0`; ARM crt0 `@0x080000C0`: IRQ sp `@0x03007FA0`, SYS sp `@0x03007E00`, WAITCNT 0x4014, DMA3 zero fill `@0x03000E7C` (0x2AA0 bytes), DMA3 copy `rom:0x7FD6BC` to `@0x03000000` (0xE7C bytes), then `@0x0802369C` veneer to `@0x08021D74` with lr = main | #boot | C | OVW |
| `@0x08021D74` runs 66 C++ static constructors (self-relative list `@0x08023588`-`@0x0802368F`); they store the vtables of the IWRAM singletons (states, forms, world) | #boot #object | C | OVW |
| main `@0x08000158`: SRAM probe, system init `@0x0801F248`, logo scenes `@0x0801EDE0` and `@0x0801D378`, then forever: intro scene `@0x0801ED3C`, set scene `@0x0801F2CE`, run `@0x0801F294` | #boot | C | OVW |
| System table `@0x087FBDAC` = {save `@0x0300389C`, video/IRQ `@0x03000E3C`, input `@0x0300229C`, sound `@0x030026BC`} | #boot | C | OVW |
| Scene manager `@0x03000E1C`: +0 current scene, +4 VBlank counter, +8 depth, +0xC stack. Scene vtable +4 enter, +8 leave, +0xC update, +0x10 VBlank, +0x14 tick (nonzero = done). Push `@0x0801F2F4` runs a nested frame loop | #boot | C | OVW |
| Frame loop `@0x0801F294`: scene update, sound `@0x08020BD8`, VBlank wait `@0x08020D40` (`swi 2` Halt; CPU load to video +0x3C), input `@0x0801EEFC`, scene tick. One game frame = one VBlank | #boot | C | OVW |
| IRQ: `@0x08020D26` sets `[@0x03007FFC]` = `@0x03000824` (ROM copy `rom:0x7FDEE0`), IE 0x2000, DISPSTAT 0x18; handler ORs the bit into `@0x03000E3C` +0x38 and calls `[@0x03000E3C + 4 * bit]` with IRQs re-enabled; Game Pak IRQ hangs (`b .` at `rom:0x7FDFAC`). VBlank callback `@0x0801F230` (counter `@0x03000E20`, scene +0x10) | #boot | C | OVW |
| SRAM probe at boot (`@0x0800015C`) and on every map load (`@0x0800C390`): writes test bytes to `0x0E000000`/`0x0E000001`, sets `[@0x03001FC0]` = 1 if they read back, restores them. Readers: ext 34, 35, 36, 37, 129. The build must never expose writable SRAM | #antipiracy #boot #decision | C (code), L (= the wiki anti-piracy check) | OVW |
| In-game scene vtable `@0x08023884` (Continue `@0x0800186E`): update `@0x08004EA8` (camera object `[@0x03000FF4]`, world update), tick `@0x08004EDC` (OAM `@0x08006AA6`, regions `@0x08006250`, menu buttons `@0x08004CAC`) | #boot #object | C (calls), L (names) | OVW |
| World `@0x03001120`: +4 count, +8 active list (128), +0x208/+0x20C draw list, +0x40C second list, +0x610 deferred refcount list, +0xBF8 menu copy, +0xBFC sound state. add `@0x080068B8` (134 callers), remove `@0x080068CA`, replace `@0x08006942`, broadcast `@0x08006DA6`, query `@0x08006E0A`, count `@0x08006E64`, update and depth sort `@0x08006AD8` | #object | C | OVW |
| Class chain: sprite root `@0x080075A8` > mover `@0x080078E4` (vt `@0x08024E84`, x/y 8.8 at +0x158/+0x15C) > combatant `@0x0800F80C` (vt `@0x08025564`) > player `@0x080086D4` (0x1A0 bytes, vt `@0x08025604`). NPC `@0x0800D6DE` (vt `@0x080257D0`) | #object #sprite | C | OVW |
| Actor vtable: +0x24 message, +0x28 set state (`@0x080088BA`: state = `[@0x083B5278 + 4n]`, reached as `@0x083B5268` + 0x10), +0x2C in state, +0x38 apply hit, +0x3C heal, +0x40 take damage | #object #state | C (player) | OVW |
| 0x20 hurt, 0x21 death, 0x23 Saiyan transform, 0x24 revert, 0x25 Namek transform are STATE ids set through vtable +0x28, not messages on the +0x24 bus (clarifies FRM "Messages" line) | #state #form | C | OVW |

The game wraps at run time: the line counter `@0x0801F7FC` (from `@0x08018A38` scan and `@0x0801963C` database, width 0xA0, font `0x0802CC64`) and `text_wrapped` `@0x0801F702` both skip leading spaces, add glyph width + 1 per byte (spaces too) and, past 160 px, end the line at the last space (or before the byte when there is none). Both end a line at `0x0A` and the text at 00. Only the counter tests `0x0B` (ends the text) and skips `0x0A` and `0x0B` at a line start; `text_wrapped` tests only 00, `0x0A` and `0x20`. While it exists the block tick spends ki every frame: accumulator += record +0x57 x 0x3277 >> 4, spend accumulator >> 16 through `0x08013504` (default +0x57 = 0x10: about 0.2 ki a frame, 12 a second); at ki 0 or when B or R is released the shield winds down. | Buu's Fury damage `0x0800E9D8` (decomp `slice_000158.s:31008` for the block test): melee uses attacker +0x1B0, ki +0x1B2; defender END from +0x1B4/+0x1B6; damage in 1/1024 HP units at hit +0x18 = (factor x dmg + 0x7F) >> 7 (factor = defender +0x1B8 melee, +0x1B9 ki). **Block:** when defender +0x1C8 is set AND (hit +0 XOR 1) = defender facing +0x10 (the hit comes from the front) AND damage is not 0, damage = damage x [defender +0x1C0] >> 16. It runs before the melee-only reflect test, so it covers melee and ki. A hit from behind or the side is not reduced | game:bf #combat | C | BLK |

Byte equal: row decoder (LoG `0x08020820`, BF `0x0803EEE4`, 0xEC bytes), note trigger (LoG `0x0801FE20`, BF `0x0803E4E4`, 0x90 bytes), note frequency table (LoG `0x087FC93C`, BF `0x087B6724`, 120 entries). Equal but for BL offsets: music_play (LoG `0x08020A4C`, BF `0x0803F110`), tick `0x0802090C`, sequencer `0x080209B8`, mixer `0x0801FA14`. Effect tables (LoG `0x087FCB1C`, BF `0x087B6904`) have 32 handlers each, equal by 40-instruction signature. Only the SFX voice copy code differs (DMA in LoG II, a copy call in BF). Both set timer 0 reload 0xFBE6 (about 15978 Hz) | #sound game:bf | C (`v2_sound_diff*.txt`, byte compare) | CH2 |
| Song record (20 bytes): +0 pattern pointer table, +4 order bytes (0xFF loops to order 0, row decoder `0x08020840`), +8 sample directory, +0xC mask table (14 bytes), +0x10 tempo (ticks: 40000 / tempo samples, `0x08020A86`-`0x08020A90`), +0x11 ticks per row (`0x08020A5C`). The player reads every song field through the record, so a song can sit anywhere | #sound | C (listings) | CH2 |
| Pattern format (row decoder `0x08020820`): byte 0 = rows; a row is events until a 0 byte; event = code << 4 \| channel 1-15; code 0 = the next byte is the channel mask (kept), code 1 = the kept mask, code 2-15 = mask table[code - 2]. Mask bit 0 note (+0x5A; 0xFF = note off), bit 1 instrument (+0x5B), bit 2 volume (+0x5C), bit 3 effect and value (+0x5D, +0x5E). Effect byte indexes the 32-entry handler table. Every song in both games decodes with this rule (44 LoG II, 53 BF; notes < 120 or 0xFF, effects < 32, instruments inside the directory) | #sound | C (`v2_song_decode.txt`) | CH2 |
| Sample directory entry (12 bytes): u32 data, u16 flags (0, 1 or 3), u16 loop start, u16 length in bytes, u16 rate (16000 typical). Samples are 8-bit and back to back: next data = data + length for all 100 LoG II gaps and all 78 BF gaps. Note trigger: entry = directory + 12 x instrument (`0x0801FE7E`-`0x0801FE86`) | #sound | C (bytes, listing) | CH2 |

Toy model (S: swing chance 0.5 a frame when ready, rows 27/28 cost 0-20 frames): one Jr 2.50 to 1.11 swings/s; 4 crowded Jrs 9.89 to 4.44 swings/s, damaging hits 1.68 to 1.34/s, median free frames after a hurt 3 to 14, Gohan from full against 27 HP/s regeneration about 33 s to 55 s. Blasts from one lined-up Jr in the ring: 69 to 50 a minute if it rolls every frame, 19 to 10 if every 8 frames; 20 to 40 rolls a blast.
